# Story Queue — live state

**API call ledger (50/month hard cap):** used **8** · remaining **42** · gate spent 8 of its ≤10 reserve · reserved for owner's real pulls ~40. No live call without a ledger entry (WORKFLOW.md §6). Detail ledger: `fixtures/live-samples/ledger.json`.

**Spent 2026-07-26 (owner-authorized):** gate run, subject `3651 S Wood St, Chicago, IL 60609` (4bd / ba `1.5:2` per owner call, 1.0mi, window 07-28→08-20, 3yr) = 6 calls, two-phase as planned. All HTTP 200, syntax verified working (`X-Total-Count` echoed). Result: **1 comp total → NO-GO**. Queue halted pending owner redesign decision.

**Planned spend (owner-authorized 2026-07-26, round 2):** broad diagnostic pull = **2 calls** (Active + Inactive): `daysOld=1:1095`, radius 2.0mi, `bedrooms=3:4` (owner-approved comp-pool expansion), propertyType `Multi-Family|Apartment|Townhouse|Single Family|Condo`, NO bathrooms filter, limit 500. Seasonal windowing applied locally to returned data; verdict on in-window distinct comps.

**Deadline: 7/29/2026** — the tool must be able to price a real unit by this date. Working beats pretty; see `PROJECT_MANAGER.md`'s "Deadline awareness."

Owned by the project manager. Every dispatch/completion/reorder is an edit here, committed to main. States: `BLOCKED · READY · DISPATCHED · IN_DEV · IN_REVIEW · REGRESSION · DONE`.

**Global rule:** everything is blocked by `T-S3` (the go/no-go gate) until it passes. `F4-S7` (harness) is built *as part of* the gate. `T-S4` (Playwright specs) is not a queue item — it rides inside every story's QA work. `T-S1` (golden files) rides with `F4-S3`.

**Lanes** (parallelism guide — dispatch parallel stories only across different lanes):
`PIPE` = data pipeline & stats · `UI` = screens/components · `INFRA` = persistence, API client, forms · `TEST` = standalone test items

| 13g | **Record a response's byte length or digest at write time, and the last cache asymmetry closes** (NEW, from F3-S4 QA verify — a solution, not a complaint) | INFRA | F3-S4 ✅ | **READY** | F3-S4 deliberately prices **post-write corruption** as *demoted but not owed* — **stuck-and-free rather than unbounded-spend**, which is D24's position and which `backend-reviewer` then showed is bounded by construction anyway (`os.replace` is atomic, so this code path cannot itself produce a truncated file). **But the two cases genuinely ARE distinguishable — just not with data we currently record.** `write_raw_response` already writes a sidecar. **Recording the response's byte length or digest at write time lets `_corroborate` ask "is this file still what we wrote?"** — **no** → real corruption, a re-fetch delivers something new, **owed**; **yes** → the response itself is unreadable, a re-fetch returns identical bytes, **not owed**. **One field, and the last asymmetry in the cache's pricing goes away.** ⚠ Note this is *not* the sidecar-`sig` idea I withdrew at row 13e — that one made the index stricter than the loader; this one adds a fact neither side currently has, and changes no trust boundary |

Real effect: the user drags the slider, an early *superseded* request 500s late, and the rust **"Re-derive failed — the numbers below are from before your last edit"** banner paints over numbers that are **correct and current**. *A false honesty warning is its own honesty problem.* **M6** — delete the `!response.ok` throw: 5/5 still pass. | 14c | **When should the "numbers are stale" banner fall — when a re-derive STARTS, or when it SUCCEEDS?** (NEW, from row 14b QA) | UI | — | **HELD — OWNER DECISION (product honesty, no AC anywhere)** | **Surfaced as mutation survivor M9, which QA deliberately declined to kill because killing it either way would have been QA legislating a user-facing honesty message.** `error` is cleared in two places; the mutant that keeps `prev.error` through the `"deriving"` reset **survives**, and it is *a defensible alternative rather than a bug*. **Today the rust banner — "Re-derive failed — the numbers below are from before your last edit" — falls the moment a retry STARTS.** But during that retry the numbers on screen **really are** still from before the last edit, so the warning is arguably still true and clearing it early asserts a freshness that does not exist. **PM's read, offered as a recommendation and not a ruling: the banner should fall on SUCCESS.** It follows from an existing principle rather than inventing one — the same shape as the `as_of` restamp ruling, where restamping on a failed fetch contradicted `as_of`'s existing meaning rather than redefining it. NORTH_STAR forbids asserting confidence the evidence does not support, and between retry-start and retry-return there is no fresh evidence. **But this is a user-facing honesty message with no AC, so the owner decides, not me.** QA's V8 was written to assert only the outcome **both** timings agree on, so it does not prejudge the answer and nothing needs rewriting either way |
| 10a | **The AST wall-clock guard's real weakness is its SCOPE, not its spellings — it never scans `models/`, where this story just put date arithmetic** | TEST | — | **READY — priority raised** | ⚠ **This row has now been corrected TWICE, and the third version is the one that matters.** (v1, mine, wrong) I recorded F4-S8's dev's claim that `__import__('datetime').date.today()` evades the guard. **Two independent agents disproved it by running `ast.parse`** — that expression is still an `ast.Attribute` with `attr == "today"`, and `ast.walk` does not care what the base resolves to. `importlib` and aliased-import forms are caught too (`imported_modules` reads `alias.name`, not `asname`). (v2) The only evading spelling anyone could construct is `getattr(date, 'to'+'day')()` — **which QA rightly calls "not an accident mode."** ⭐ **(v3, the real finding, from F4-S8's QA): the guard scans `pipeline` and `stats` ONLY — and `removed_on` was just added to `models/domain.py`, which it does not scan at all.** So this story's new date arithmetic sits **entirely outside the structural guard**, not because of a clever bypass but because nobody pointed the guard at that directory. **Fix: extend the scan to `models/`.** Chasing exotic spellings was the wrong lead; **a guard's blind spot is usually a directory, not a syntax trick.** The story is nonetheless safe — QA measured the behavioural nets from both sides (a `date.today()` mutant in `_build_comp` kills **19** tests; the `days_since_removal` wall-clock mutant kills **3**, including one that uses two *historical* pull dates a year apart so a wall-clock implementation can never accidentally agree and the test cannot decay) | ⚠ **THIS ROW WAS WRONG WHEN I WROTE IT AND `backend-reviewer` CORRECTED IT BY EXECUTION.** I recorded F4-S8's dev's claim that `__import__('datetime').date.today()` evades the guard. **It does not.** The reviewer ran `ast.parse` on it: that expression is **still an `ast.Attribute` node with `attr == "today"`**, and `ast.walk` does not care what the base expression resolves to, so `CLOCK_ATTRIBUTES` catches it. **The real gap is string-key indirection: `getattr(date, "today")()` produces no `Attribute` named `today` and no `Name` node, and is NOT caught.** (Aliased imports are fine — `import time as _time` is still caught, because `imported_modules` uses `alias.name`, not `alias.asname`.) **Pre-existing in `test_derivation_graph.py`; F4-S8 introduced nothing.** The guard still matters — it enforces the owner's `as_of`-is-the-pull-date ruling, so reopening an old workspace shows the numbers it showed when pulled. Fix: extend to `getattr` string-key reach-throughs, **and record in the docstring that structural enforcement is a backstop to behavioural coverage, never a replacement.** **Two lessons, and the second is about me:** the D5 structural regex has a proven hole too (a plain `w / total` walks through it), so **two guards, two holes, and in both cases a behavioural test was what actually held** — *and* **I propagated an unverified agent claim into a queue row without probing it.** Dispatch text and queue rows are not exempt from the probe-don't-assume rule; this is the third wrong detail I have sourced tonight | **A guard with a hole is worse than no guard, because it is *believed*.** `test_pipeline_and_stats_cannot_read_the_wall_clock` enforces one of this project's load-bearing owner rulings — **`as_of` is the pull date, not the wall clock**, so that reopening an old workspace shows the same numbers it showed when pulled. The guard was built as a structural, AST-level enforcement precisely so the rule could not be violated by accident. **It does its job on the obvious spelling (verified: a plain `date.today()` produces 7 failures including the guard) and is blind to the dynamic one.** F4-S8's dev found it because its *behavioural* tests caught what the guard missed — the same lesson as the D5 structural regex, which F5-S2 proved a plain `w / total` walks straight through. **Two structural guards on this project now have proven holes, and in both cases a behavioural test was the thing that actually held.** Fix: extend the AST check to `__import__`/`getattr`/`importlib` reach-throughs, **and record in the test's own docstring that structural enforcement is a backstop to behavioural coverage, never a replacement** — that framing is what stops the next agent trusting it |

QA did not shrug it off as someone else's problem: it passed the spec alone (5 passed), passed it in the adjacent pair three consecutive times (21 passed), and passed it on both later full runs. **Mechanism established: four specs still hardcode port 8000 — `f0-s1b-integration`, `f1-s2-recents`, `f2-s1-search-form`, `ws1-results-view` — and run back-to-back. `serverProcess.kill()` is ASYNCHRONOUS, so the next spec's bind can fail while `waitForServer` succeeds against the PREVIOUS, DYING server — which has a different `RENTCOMP_HOME`.** ⭐ **That is the WORKFLOW §2 contention failure occurring WITHIN OUR OWN SUITE rather than across agents, which nobody had considered — and this time it surfaced as a false RED, the lucky direction.** The unlucky direction is a false green, and nothing about the mechanism prefers one. **Second time this port has produced a wrong answer during a real gate.** Fix: migrate all four to the ephemeral-port uvicorn pattern now in `WORKFLOW.md` §2 (`python -m uvicorn rentcomp.app:app --port <ephemeral>`, plus the live-child + banner-names-our-port identity check). Scope: drop the marker from the five specs that carry it, confirm the suite still passes under the config's own `workers: 1` |

| 37c | **"The recents index never derives" has no regression pin — and F1-S1's dev is the most likely person to break it** (NEW, from F1-S2 QA verify) | INFRA | F1-S2 ✅ | **DONE** | merged; **PM re-ran pytest on the main checkout: `1591 passed / 3 documented platform failures / 1 skipped / 3 xfailed`** = baseline 1585 + 6, nothing else moved. **No dev phase — the behaviour was already correct and merely unpinned**, and all six tests passed on arrival against unmodified `main`. ⭐ **MY SKETCH WAS VACUOUS AND QA PROVED IT BY MEASUREMENT.** I proposed monkeypatching `rentcomp.storage.pulls.load_shaped_pull`. But `api/derive.py:29` does `from rentcomp.storage.pulls import load_shaped_pull`, and **a module-level `from`-import binds the function OBJECT into the consumer's namespace at import time**, so patching the defining module is invisible to exactly the caller style a developer would write. QA mutated `_row()` with a per-row anchor in its most likely form and ran three things: **its own tests FAILED (correct), the other 154 F1-S2 tests all PASSED (confirming the row's premise), and my sketch — written as a throwaway spec — PASSED, vacuous against the exact change it exists to catch.** Every door is now armed on **both** sides: `raising=True` on the defining module (so a rename fails loudly rather than silently disarming) and `raising=False` on the consumer namespace. **Six doors, not one** — including `pipeline.shape.shape_raw_pull_with_summary`, because F4-S4 made `shape_raw_pull` a thin delegating wrapper, so pinning the wrapper alone leaves the bottom of the stack open. `read_manifest`/`raw_response_paths`/`_evidence_version` deliberately **not** armed — they are the cheap path `pull_exists` is built on, and arming them would forbid the correct implementation. Tripwires **record then raise**, with assertions against the recorded list, so a caller that swallows the raise is still caught; `DerivationAttempted` subclasses **`BaseException`** because `pull_exists` and the workspace store both catch broad tuples and a swallowable tripwire would turn "this route derives" into "this row is broken," which is quieter than no test | **`backend-reviewer` confirmed the property by one-off execution, and QA's point is that a one-off execution is not a test.** `test_the_workspace_route_cannot_reach_the_source_at_all` pins *no network*; `pull_exists`'s docstring pins *no shaping*; **nothing fails if someone adds `load_shaped_pull` to `_row()`.** And that is not a hypothetical: **F1-S1 builds the Home view, whose recents rows the epic describes as carrying an anchor — deriving one per row is the natural way to get it**, and it would blow the epic's "<1s, zero API calls" budget silently, because deriving is free of network but not of time. **This is the "<1s" criterion in its only testable form** — no wall-clock assertion exists anywhere and QA declined to add one as machine-dependent and flaky, which was right. Fix is one L2 test: monkeypatch `rentcomp.storage.pulls.load_shaped_pull` to raise, assert `GET /api/workspaces` still returns 200. **Land it before F1-S1 is dispatched so the trap is armed before the foot arrives** |
| 38 | F1-S1 home view | UI | F1-S2 ✅, 37c ✅ | **READY — but DECIDE THE ANCHOR COLUMN AT DISPATCH, not in a round trip** | ⚠ **The tension is unresolved in the CONTRACT, not merely in the code, and F1-S1's developer will hit it in its first hour.** Epic F1 flow step 1 names the recents table as `address, specs, radius, **anchor**, age`. `RecentWorkspace` (`models/responses.py:589`) is `extra="forbid"` **with no anchor field**, and its docstring explicitly punts: *"Serving an `anchor` column here would mean deriving every saved workspace on Home's mount… **F1-S1 decides what to do about that column.**"* **So the dev opens the epic, sees an anchor column, queries the index, finds no anchor — and the cheapest-looking fix is the expensive one.** Row 37c now makes that **fail loudly** instead of silently, which is exactly why it was sequenced first. **Three candidate answers: drop the column (spec change → owner); snapshot the anchor at save time and read it off the workspace file; or derive on demand per visible row (expensive, and 37c forbids it at the index).** **PM recommendation: snapshot at save time.** It is consistent with F1's own language — *"restored exactly as last left"* — so a recents row would show precisely the number that workspace last showed, which is a real number that was true rather than a fresh derivation dressed as history. That follows an existing definition rather than redefining one. **But it decides what a displayed number means, so I am flagging it to the owner rather than ruling it.** ⚠ **37c deliberately pins the MECHANISM and never the response's field list** — its QA declined to assert "no `anchor` key", because that would forbid the snapshot answer along with the expensive one. **That latitude is deliberate; do not let a later story pin the field list and close it off** |
| 45 | **Five `pipeline/` modules construct wire-contract models directly from `models/responses.py`** (NEW, observed by `backend-reviewer` on F4-S5, scope widened by PM probe) | INFRA | — | **READY — low priority, architectural clarification not a defect** | **The reviewer flagged `cohorts.py` importing `CohortStat` from `responses.py` as "the established, if slightly unusual, shape of this stage" and explicitly NOT a new violation — correct, F4-S5 introduced nothing.** ⚠ **But I probed the scope before recording it and it is wider than one module: `anchor.py` (Anchor, Band), `buckets.py` (Anchor, Band, BucketId, BucketStat), `cohorts.py` (CohortStat), `derive.py` and `pricetest.py` all import from `models/responses.py`.** Six pipeline modules import `models/domain.py` as expected; five *also* reach forward to the response layer. **Why it is worth a row rather than a shrug: `CLAUDE.md`'s hard rule says "three model layers stay separate — `dto.py` (wire truth) → `domain.py` (our truth) → `responses.py` (API contract)… don't let a DTO leak past the pipeline boundary."** That rule is written for the *forward* direction; **this is the reverse — the API contract reaching backwards into derivation** — and it is unaddressed by the rule as written. **Consequence, which is what makes it real: a wire-shape change (e.g. row 12b's `comp_keys` rename or `pulled_comp_keys` addition) edits a file that five pipeline stages import, so "regenerate the TS types" is never the whole blast radius.** Scope: decide whether this is ratified as intentional (and D5's wording amended to say so) or genuinely wants an intermediate type — **an ADR-shaped question, not a refactor to start unilaterally.** Do not let a story "fix" it in passing |

| **V2: measured leave-one-out influence — the number `contribution_share` only pretends to be** | row 14a write-up, resolution (F) | The genuinely appealing option, deliberately **not** taken now. It is what the current NORTH_STAR gloss actually promises, and §2.4 of the write-up contains a working prototype built from the existing pipeline. Three real obstacles: naive full-derive LOO over 484 comps measured **~3.2 s** (6.6 ms × 484), blowing F5-S2's 150 ms debounce; a median's true influence is a lumpy **"$0 or $128"** figure, not a percentage, and five-of-nine rows reading `$0.00` **invites the user to delete comps that are holding the median's rank** — a new misreading of the same shape as the WS-1a `too_few_in_range` guard; and "which aggregate?" recurs (2–4 numbers per comp, since anchor / bucket-membership / bucket-outcome / kNN each have a different evidence set). **Taking the (E) rename now reserves the honest name for the honest number.** Becomes the point rather than a follow-up if the owner says "which comp is actually driving my price?" is what he wants answered while curating |

| **Secrets are not 0600 on Windows — security gap, not cosmetic** | 2026-07-28 Windows bring-up | `storage/secrets.py` builds its 0600 guarantee from `os.open(..., 0o600)` + `os.chmod`. On Windows CPython those only toggle the read-only attribute; group/other bits are meaningless, so `~/.rentcomp/secrets.json` lands 0666. The 2 failing tests (`test_rentcast_client.py::test_the_key_round_trips_and_the_file_is_0600`, `test_rentcast_client_dev.py::test_saving_twice_leaves_one_key_and_still_0600`) are correctly detecting a real hole, not being wrong. Fix needs an ACL path (`icacls`/`pywin32`) behind a `sys.platform` branch, or an explicit documented "POSIX only" stance. **Matters before any Windows machine holds a real key** |
| `~` in `RENTCOMP_HOME` resolves differently on Windows | 2026-07-28 Windows bring-up | `os.path.expanduser` prefers `USERPROFILE` over `HOME` on Windows, so `test_config_store_dev.py::test_home_override_expands_a_tilde` (which sets `HOME`) resolves outside its tmp_path. Test-portability plus a real behavioural difference in the documented override. Passes on Linux |
| **⚠ Playwright reports FALSE GREEN inside every isolated worktree — `13 passed, 10 skipped`, exit 0** | F11-S2 dev finding, 2026-07-28 | **The `f6f254e` false-green class has reappeared through a different door.** `e2e/tests/support/local-server.ts` resolves `.venv` from `REPO_ROOT`, which inside a worktree is *the worktree* — where no `.venv` exists — so the precondition check bails and all 10 live-server specs skip **while the run still exits 0.** Every `isolation: "worktree"` dispatch is affected, which as of today is how most agents run. The F11-S2 dev caught it, worked around it with a gitignored directory junction `<worktree>/.venv → <main>/.venv`, and **verified the spawned server loaded its own code before trusting the green** — exactly right. **Needs a real fix** (resolve `.venv` from the main checkout via `git rev-parse --git-common-dir`, or fail loudly instead of skipping). Until then every worktree dispatch must say so, and no agent may report 13-passed as green. **A skip that exits 0 is indistinguishable from a pass, which is why this keeps recurring — the fix should make the precondition failure LOUD, not merely correct** |
| **`models/dto.py` has never been built and no story owns it** | backend-reviewer on F4-S2, 2026-07-28 | `dto.py`'s own docstring says "Populated by **F4-S2** against `fixtures/live-samples/` ground truth" — but F4-S2's actual AC (stories doc lines 77–78) says nothing about it, and `shape.py` parses raw RentCast dicts directly (a `[DEFAULT]` already logged at WS-1: "raw dicts parsed directly, no formal `dto.py` layer yet"). So the file is a stub whose docstring reads as an unmet commitment. **CLAUDE.md's three-model-layer rule (dto → domain → responses) is currently two layers in practice.** Same unowned-gap class as `/api/config`, the Settings UI, and the pull orchestrator — all of which needed a PM assignment. Decide: assign `dto.py` to a story, or ratify the two-layer reality and fix the docstring |
| **Should `effective_dom == 0` be representable at all?** | F11-S2 QA finding, 2026-07-28 | `StitchedComp.effective_dom` is `ge=0`, so a listing removed on its own listing date reaches the KM estimator as a genuine **day-0 event**, making S(0) < 1 and putting the curve's left boundary below 1 before any time has passed. PM ruled the estimator must **report what the data says** rather than clamp in `stats/` — but a same-day list-and-remove is far more likely a mis-entry or an instant withdrawal than a real 0-day lease, which makes this a **removal-classification question for F4-S3/F4-S8**, not an estimator one. Decide whether DOM is clamped to ≥1 at the pipeline boundary, or whether DOM-0 records are classified out entirely. Unknown whether any exist in the current pull — check before deciding |
| **The documented full-gate command line is broken in all 3 legs — and the vitest leg is RED, not merely absent** | F4-S1 dev + QA, sharpened by F4-S2 QA, 2026-07-28 | **Sharpened 2026-07-28:** `cd frontend && npx vitest run` **exits 1** ("No test files found"), so the middle leg of `pytest && npx vitest run && npx playwright test` is a hard failure *today*, not a benign no-op — the `useDerive` spec (F5-S2) does not exist yet. Every prior "vitest N/A, not a regression" note in this log was therefore describing a red leg as absent. Nothing is broken in product code, but the three-layer gate **cannot currently pass as written**, which matters at the FINAL pass (#43) and in `/test`. Needs `--passWithNoTests` until F5-S2 lands, plus the cwd fixes below |
| **The documented full-gate command line does not work from the repo root — 2 of its 3 commands fail** | F4-S1 dev + QA findings, 2026-07-28 | `npx vitest run` from root collects `e2e/tests/*.spec.ts` and Playwright refuses them (4 suites fail); `npx playwright test` from root errors with a `@playwright/test` version conflict + "No tests found" (there is no root `package.json`). Working invocations are `cd frontend && npx vitest run` (reports no test files — none exist yet) and `cd e2e && npx playwright test` (23 passed). **CLAUDE.md's and WORKFLOW.md's gate line reads `pytest && npx vitest run && npx playwright test`, so anyone running it literally from root gets 2 false reds.** Cosmetic today because everyone has been running them per-directory, but it becomes a real trap at the FINAL regression pass (#43) and in `/test` — the same false-signal class as the Windows E2E skip. Wants documented cwds or a root runner |
| **`SKILLS_MAP.md` assigns skills that are not installed on this machine — the whole skills mechanism is a no-op** | F4-S1 dev finding + PM verification, 2026-07-28 | The dev went looking for its mandatory `engineering:code-review`, found only `code-review:code-review` (a GitHub-PR workflow orchestrating subagents via `gh` — no PR here, wrong tool), and ran the review substance manually. **PM then verified by filesystem search: no `engineering/`, `operations/`, or `product-management/` skill directory exists anywhere under `~/.claude`.** So every skill named for all three roles is unresolvable — `engineering:{code-review,debug,architecture,testing-strategy,deploy-checklist}`, `operations:status-report`, `product-management:*`, `data:*`. Outcomes have held anyway (agents did the substance by hand; F0-S2's ADR got written without `engineering:architecture`), so this is **not** a correctness finding — but PROJECT_MANAGER.md's "skills travel with the dispatch" has been ceremony for the entire project, and the near-miss name is the real hazard: a less careful agent would have followed the GitHub-PR workflow into a repo with no PRs. Owner decision: install the skills, rewrite SKILLS_MAP to name real equivalents, or drop the mechanism |

| 2026-07-29 | PM | **RULING on `frontend-reviewer`'s one low-severity nit — acceptable as-is, not queued, and the reasoning matters more than the verdict.** `CompList`'s header renders `"{includedCount} included of {comps.length} shown"`: `includedCount` is server-sourced (`breakdown.included`) but the second number is the length of the locally-filtered `visible` array. The reviewer correctly spotted this is **the same shape as the footer count that WAS fixed** to use `breakdown.filtered`, and asked for a consistency decision. **Ruling: leave it.** No `breakdown` field exists for a visible count, so the alternatives are (a) count the array the view was handed, or (b) synthesise it client-side as `included + excluded` — **and (b) is arithmetic on server numbers, which is precisely what D5 forbids.** The footer differs because `breakdown.filtered` **already exists**; where the field exists, use it, and the fix was right. **The general rule this settles: counting a list you were GIVEN is not deriving a statistic; combining numbers you were given is. D5's line falls between those two, not at "any number computed in TypeScript."** Recording it because the distinction will recur at F9-S1 and F10-S2, and getting it wrong in the strict direction would push the view into demanding a bespoke `breakdown` field per rendered count |
